Kapitel mit „notApplicable erlaubt“ dürfen entfallen: Zeigt deine Recherche, dass es das Thema in „Suncraft“ NICHT gibt (z. B. Gegner/Bosse in einem Aufbau- oder Fabrikspiel ohne Kampf) oder nur so am Rande, dass ein eigenes Kapitel sinnlos ist, schreibe das Kapitel NICHT, erkläre nicht lang, dass es fehlt, und fülle es nicht mit Stoff aus anderen Themen auf. Antworte für dieses Kapitel stattdessen mit diesem Objekt (in einem eigenen Codeblock wie jedes andere Kapitel): { "game": "Suncraft", "steamAppId": 4078110, "chapter": "K05", "notApplicable": true, "reason": "..." } – „reason“ ist ein kurzer Satz, warum (was das Spiel stattdessen hat). Kapitel mit „notApplicable NICHT erlaubt“ schreibst du immer vollständig.

Sonderfall Komplettlösung (K01): Beantworte dieses Kapitel ALLEIN in einer eigenen Antwort (es ist lang), nicht zusammen mit anderen Kapiteln. Der Pflicht-Aufbau oben gilt dafür NICHT; es gilt stattdessen:
Prüfe zuerst, ob es eine belastbare, VOLLSTÄNDIGE Quelle für eine Komplettlösung gibt (große Seite, offizieller Guide, großes Wiki). Wenn nicht: gib "sections" mit EINEM
"alert" (variant "danger") zurück, der sagt, dass es (Stand heute) keine belastbare Quelle für eine Komplettlösung gibt – erfinde nie eine Komplettlösung.
Sonst: das ganze Spiel in der Reihenfolge des Spiels, jedes Story-Kapitel bzw. jede Mission als ein Teil mit dem Aufbau unten (Teil-Überschrift level 1,
Überblick, Gebiete mit level 2, Boss-Karten, typisierte Hinweise genau dort, wo sie wichtig werden, die vollständigen Rätsel-Lösungen). Halte es KOMPAKT
genug für EINE Antwort (Hauptroute, nur die wichtigen Hinweise, kein Füllstoff).
Aufbau JEDES Teils (wie ein offizielles gedrucktes Lösungsbuch – nur der Aufbau, nie dessen Text):
1. "heading" mit "level": 1 = der Teil (Story-Kapitel/Mission); in "htmlContent" EIN kurzer Orientierungssatz: wo du bist und worum es in diesem Teil geht.
2. "overview" „Auf einen Blick“: empfohlenes Level, wie viele Sammelobjekte/Truhen der Teil hat ("counts"), die Erfolge dieses Teils (API-Namen),
   wichtige Gegenstände ("keyItems"), was verpassbar ist ("missables") und ob der Teil an einem Point of no Return endet ("endsAtPointOfNoReturn").
3. Dann je GEBIET/ORT in der Reihenfolge, in der man durchkommt:
   a. "heading" mit "level": 2 = Name des Gebiets; in "htmlContent" ein Orientierungssatz für dieses Gebiet.
   b. Die Route: "story" mit "variant": "steps".
   c. Was es in diesem Gebiet zu finden gibt – nur wenn es dort so etwas gibt: eine "checklist_table" mit den Truhen/Sammelobjekten/Gegenständen dieses
      Gebiets (abhakbar, eindeutige "itemId", eine Spalte „Verpassbar“).
   d. Nebenquests, die hier verfügbar werden: EIN kompakter "alert" mit "kind": "side_quests" (siehe Nebenquest-Regel) – andere optionale Inhalte
      (optionaler Boss, Zusatzgebiet): "alert" mit "kind": "optional".
   e. Ein Bosskampf: eine "boss"-Karte direkt nach dem Schritt, der zum Kampf führt.
   f. Ein Erfolg, der hier freigeschaltet wird: "alert" mit "kind": "achievement" und "apiName".
4. Am Ende des Teils – vor einem Point of no Return – ein "alert" mit "kind": "point_of_no_return" und "items" (was du vorher erledigen musst).
Lesefluss (verbindliche Regel):
- Jeder Schritt sind 1–2 vollständige Sätze in der Du-Form, z. B. „Geh ins Dorf Kol und übernachte im Gasthaus.“ – kein Telegrammstil, kein „Ziel:“,
  keine aus dem Spiel oder aus Quellen kopierten Ziel-Listen.
- Fasse triviale Ziele (Weg, Gespräch, Zwischensequenz) zu einem Schritt zusammen. Keine eigene Nummerierung.
- Typisierte Hinweise (verpassbar, Point of no Return, Erfolg, optional, wichtig, Tipp) stehen als eigener "alert" genau dort, wo sie wichtig werden – nie im Schritt-Text.
Erlaubte Bausteine (nur diese Typen):
- "heading": { "type": "heading", "level": 1, "title": "Name des Teils oder Gebiets", "htmlContent": "ein Orientierungssatz" } ("level" 1 = Teil, 2 = Gebiet)
- "overview": „Auf einen Blick“ am Anfang eines Teils – { "type": "overview", "title": "Auf einen Blick", "recommendedLevel": "z. B. 10–15",
    "counts": [ { "label": "Schatztruhen", "count": 12 } ], "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "keyItems": ["..."], "missables": ["..."],
    "endsAtPointOfNoReturn": false } ("count" nur mit belegter Zahl, sonst weglassen)
- "story" mit "variant": "steps": die Route als Schritte – { "type": "story", "variant": "steps", "paragraphs": ["Geh zu ... und ...", "Danach ..."] }
  (ein Schritt pro Eintrag, keine eigene Nummerierung).
- "checklist_table": die Funde eines Gebiets (siehe „Aufbau“ 3c), gleiche Form wie in den anderen Kapiteln, mit eindeutiger "itemId" je Zeile.
- "alert" mit einer "kind": ein Hinweis GENAU an der Stelle, an der er wichtig wird – { "type": "alert", "kind": "must_have | missable | optional | tip | achievement | point_of_no_return | side_quests",
  "title": "kurze Überschrift", "htmlContent": "reiner Text" }:
  • "must_have" (Wichtig – unbedingt mitnehmen): ein Item, eine Ausrüstung, Fähigkeit oder ein Begleiter, der für den Durchlauf oder für 100 % wichtig ist – mit genauem Fundort (wo und wie man es bekommt) und warum.
  • "missable" (Verpassbar ab hier): ab diesem Punkt geht etwas verloren (zeitlich begrenzte Inhalte) – was genau, bis wann; empfiehl vorher zu speichern.
  • "optional" (Optional hier verfügbar): optionaler Boss, Zusatzgebiet, Sammelobjekt, das an dieser Stelle verfügbar wird – und wie man es startet.
  • "side_quests" (Nebenquests hier) mit "quests": die Nebenquests, die hier verfügbar werden, kompakt (Name, ab wann, Zeitfenster, kurze Belohnung).
  • "tip" (Tipp): praktischer Rat für diese Stelle (Abkürzung, Speicherpunkt, was man vorbereiten sollte).
  • "achievement" (Erfolg hier) mit "apiName": der exakte Steam-API-Name – was du hier tun musst, um ihn freizuschalten.
  • "point_of_no_return" (Point of no Return) mit "items": ["..."] – alles, was du vorher erledigen musst.
- "boss": Boss-Karte genau an der Stelle des Kampfes (nur Text) – { "type": "boss", "bossId": "eindeutige-kebab-id", "title": "Name des Bosses",
    "location": "Wo der Kampf stattfindet", "recommendedLevel": "z. B. Stufe 25", "hp": [ { "value": "z. B. 12.000", "appliesTo": "Schwierigkeit/Phase/Version",
    "source": "Name der Quelle" } ], "weaknesses": ["..."], "resistances": ["..."], "immunities": ["..."],
    "attacks": [ { "name": "...", "note": "was sie tut, wie man ausweicht" } ], "phases": ["..."], "party": "empfohlene Gruppe/Ausrüstung",
    "tactics": ["Schritt 1 ...", "Schritt 2 ..."], "rewards": ["..."], "missable": false, "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "source": "Name der Quelle" }
- "puzzle": Rätsel/Reihenfolge mit versteckter Lösung – { "type": "puzzle", "title": "Name des Rätsels", "htmlContent": "Die Aufgabe in 1-2 Sätzen, ohne Lösung",
    "puzzle": { "itemId": "eindeutige-id", "location": "Wo genau", "requirements": "Was vorher / ab wann", "missable": false,
      "hints": ["vager Hinweis", "konkreterer Hinweis"], "steps": ["Schritt 1 ...", "Schritt 2 ..."], "source": "Name der Quelle" } }
- "sources": als LETZTER Abschnitt des Kapitels die Seiten, auf die du dich wirklich gestützt hast – { "type": "sources", "sources": [ { "title": "Name der Seite", "url": "https://..." } ] }.
  Nur echte, vollständige URLs, die du tatsächlich gesehen hast; erfinde nie eine URL – ohne URL lass den Eintrag weg.
Ein Item, das in einem anderen Kapitel dieses Guides eine eigene Zeile hat (Waffen, Sammelobjekte, ...): nenne es im Hinweis und verweise auf das Kapitel („siehe Kapitel ...“), statt die Tabelle zu wiederholen.
Urheberrecht: Schreibe in EIGENEN WORTEN. Übernimm nie längere Passagen einer Quelle wörtlich; Fakten (Orte, Namen, Reihenfolgen, Codes) sind in Ordnung.

Bosse (verbindliche Regel):
- Jeder Bosskampf und jeder besonders schwere Pflichtkampf bekommt eine eigene "boss"-Karte – in der Komplettlösung direkt nach dem Schritt, der zum
  Kampf führt; in anderen Kapiteln (Bestiarium, Bosse, Beschaffungswege) dort, wo der Kampf hingehört.
- Zahlen nur mit Beleg: Jeder HP-Eintrag nennt den Wert, wofür er gilt ("appliesTo": Schwierigkeit, Phase, Version, NG+) und die Quelle ("source").
  Ohne belegten Wert schreibe { "value": "Nicht dokumentiert" } – erfinde nie Zahlen. Lass jedes Feld weg, zu dem du nichts Belegtes weißt.
- "tactics": die Taktik als Schritte in ganzen Sätzen; "achievements": nur exakte Steam-API-Namen aus der Erfolgsliste (falls es eine gibt), sonst weglassen.
- "missable": true, wenn der Kampf (oder seine Belohnung) verloren gehen kann. Kündige einen Überraschungskampf (ohne Vorwarnung im Spiel) VORHER mit einem "alert" an.

Beschaffungswege (verbindliche Regel): Für alles, was man nicht einfach aufhebt – Waffen, legendäre/ultimative Ausrüstung, Beschwörungen/Esper,
besondere Gegenstände, Belohnungen von Questreihen –, beschreibe die VOLLSTÄNDIGE Kette in der richtigen Reihenfolge: die Voraussetzungen und ab
wann es verfügbar ist, was gesammelt werden muss, wohin man geht (z. B. „die Halle, in der man die Waffe auswählt“) und JEDEN nötigen Kampf – auch
einen überraschenden, schweren Kampf am Ende (warne VOR dem Kampf und gib ihm eine "boss"-Karte). Kein Schritt darf fehlen, sonst steht der Spieler
unvorbereitet vor einem Kampf, den er nicht kennt. Ein Schritt pro Eintrag ("story" mit "variant": "steps" oder bei einem Rätsel "puzzle.steps"), die Belohnung am Ende.
Die offiziellen Steam-Erfolge (API-Name | Titel | Bedingung) – nutze für "apiName" und "achievements" NUR diese Namen:
ACH_FIRST | Erster Schritt | Beende das erste Kapitel.
ACH_SECRET | Geheimnis |

Content: as complete and concrete as possible – real names, locations, conditions, numerical values, orders.
Structure (mandatory, in this order):

Acquisition paths (mandatory rule): for everything that needs more than picking it up – weapons, legendary/ultimate gear, summons/espers,

Bosses (mandatory rule):

  location and requirements as far as they are verified. NEVER invent codes, positions or orders.

- Step 1 – before all other sources: look for a wiki dedicated to THIS game, made by fans or official (for example the game's Fandom wiki, an official

  found it; if there is none, skip this step without mentioning it. Never invent a wiki URL.

  disagree; write in your own words and never copy their text; list every wiki page you used in the chapter's "sources" section (where the answer has one).

  Game8 (walkthroughs), Fextralife (wikis), PowerPyx (roadmaps, collectibles), PSNProfiles and PlayStationTrophies.org (roadmaps, missables),

  named in a compact "alert" with "kind": "side_quests" at the spot where it becomes available – { "type": "alert", "kind": "side_quests", "title": "Side quests here",

Content: as complete and concrete as possible – real names, locations, conditions, numerical values, orders.
Structure (mandatory, in this order):
1. An "alert" (variant "gold") "Key points first": the 3–5 things you absolutely have to know about this topic.
2. An "alert" (variant "danger") with everything that is MISSABLE – or explicitly "Nothing missable".
3. "cards_grid" with strategies/recommendations from practice (e.g. early / middle / endgame, best build, best order).
4. One or more COMPLETE "checklist_table" with all entries; mandatory columns in addition to the topic-
   specific ones: "Available from" (chapter/area/level) and "Missable" (yes/no + until when).
Use the fitting building blocks:
- "alert": important warnings (variant "danger" for missables, "gold" for expert tips, "crystal" for info, "emerald" for achievements)
- "cards_grid": strategies, builds, tactics (columns 2 or 3; every card with bullets)
- "checklist_table": everything tickable (collectibles, quests, weapons, bosses ...), choose columns to fit the topic.
  Every row needs a unique, descriptive "itemId" in kebab-case (e.g. "weapon-mantis-blades"), "filterCategory"
  must appear in "categories"; "checkable" is true if the player can do/collect something.
- "puzzle": a riddle/sequence with a hidden solution – { "type": "puzzle", "title": "Name of the puzzle", "htmlContent": "The task in 1-2 sentences, no solution",
    "puzzle": { "itemId": "unique-id", "location": "Where exactly", "requirements": "What first / from when", "missable": false,
      "hints": ["vague hint", "more concrete hint"], "steps": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "source": "Name of the source" } }

    "location": "Where the fight takes place", "recommendedLevel": "e.g. level 25", "hp": [ { "value": "e.g. 12,000", "appliesTo": "difficulty/phase/version",
    "source": "Name of the source" } ], "weaknesses": ["..."], "resistances": ["..."], "immunities": ["..."],

    "tactics": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "rewards": ["..."], "missable": false, "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "source": "Name of the source" }
- "sources": as the LAST section of the chapter the pages you really relied on – { "type": "sources", "sources": [ { "title": "Name of the page", "url": "https://..." } ] }.
  Only real, complete URLs you have actually seen; never invent a URL – without a URL leave the entry out.

Acquisition paths (mandatory rule): for everything that needs more than picking it up – weapons, legendary/ultimate gear, summons/espers,
special items, rewards of quest chains – describe the COMPLETE chain in the right order: the prerequisites and from when it is available,
what has to be collected, where to go (e.g. "the hall where you choose the weapon") and EVERY required fight – including a surprising, hard
fight at the end (warn about it BEFORE the fight and give it a "boss" card). No step may be missing, otherwise the player walks into a fight he
does not know about. One step per entry ("story" with "variant": "steps", or "puzzle.steps" for a riddle), the reward at the end.

Bosses (mandatory rule):
- Every boss fight and every especially hard mandatory fight gets its own "boss" card – in the walkthrough directly after the step that leads to the
  fight; in other chapters (bestiary, bosses, acquisition paths) where the fight belongs.
- Numbers only with evidence: every HP entry names the value, what it applies to ("appliesTo": difficulty, phase, version, NG+) and its source
  ("source"). Without a verified value write { "value": "Not documented" } – never invent numbers. Leave out every field you have nothing verified for.
- "tactics": the tactic as steps in whole sentences; "achievements": only exact Steam API names from the achievement list (if there is one), otherwise leave it out.
- "missable": true if the fight (or its reward) can be lost. Announce a surprise fight (no warning in the game) with an "alert" BEFORE it.

Puzzles and riddles (mandatory rule):
- EVERY puzzle, riddle, code, sequence, combination, hidden mechanism or task of the kind "do X in the right order" gets its own "puzzle"
  section – never just a vague sentence in a table cell, an alert or a card. Leave such a table cell short and name the puzzle there.
- WHERE exactly: "puzzle.location" (area, landmark, how to get there), "puzzle.requirements" (what has to be done or owned first, when it
  becomes available) and "puzzle.missable" (true if it can be lost). The visible task goes into "htmlContent" (one or two sentences, NO solution in it).
- The COMPLETE SOLUTION in "puzzle.steps": every step in the exact order, with the concrete answers, codes, symbols, positions, items and
  places – a player must be able to solve it with the list alone. Forbidden are sentences such as "solve the riddle" or "collect the 3
  fragments in the right order" without saying where each one is and which order is the right one.
- Optional "puzzle.hints": 1 to 3 hints from vague to concrete (no complete solution), so a player can ask for help bit by bit.
- "puzzle.source": where the solution comes from (site or guide name).
- If your research cannot verify the solution: leave "puzzle.steps" EMPTY (the app then shows "Solution not documented") and still give
  location and requirements as far as they are verified. NEVER invent codes, positions or orders.
- "puzzle.itemId" (kebab-case, unique) when the player can tick the puzzle off as done.

Platform (mandatory rule): the player plays the STEAM version on PC.
- Achievements are Steam achievements. Never write about PlayStation trophies, a platinum trophy, Xbox achievements or Gamerscore;
  "100%" means all Steam achievements (plus the in-game completion they need).

Language (mandatory rule): the whole guide is in English – every sentence, heading, table cell, step, objective and instruction.

  sentences, objectives or instructions, and never repeated. Chapter titles, headings and tab names get no bracketed addition.

  objectives or quest texts) – rewrite them in English.

  (see "English version according to Steam" in the facts; without that line check it yourself). If there is no English version, use the in-game name

  sentences, objectives or instructions, and never repeated. Chapter titles, headings and tab names get no bracketed addition.

- Step 1 – before all other sources: look for a wiki dedicated to THIS game, made by fans or official (for example the game's Fandom wiki, an official
  game wiki, or a fan wiki of the kind of ffresonance.wiki for Final Fantasy Resonance – many games have none). If it has an English version (often
  the default without a language path, otherwise a language switcher or a path like /en/), use that one. Name a wiki only when your search really
  found it; if there is none, skip this step without mentioning it. Never invent a wiki URL.
  Such wikis are often fan sites: cross-check numbers (HP, levels, drop rates, prices) with a second source where one exists and say so when they

Content: as complete and concrete as possible – real names, locations, conditions, numerical values, orders.
Structure (mandatory, in this order):
1. An "alert" (variant "gold") "Key points first": the 3–5 things you absolutely have to know about this topic.
2. An "alert" (variant "danger") with everything that is MISSABLE – or explicitly "Nothing missable".
3. "cards_grid" with strategies/recommendations from practice (e.g. early / middle / endgame, best build, best order).
4. One or more COMPLETE "checklist_table" with all entries; mandatory columns in addition to the topic-
   specific ones: "Available from" (chapter/area/level) and "Missable" (yes/no + until when).
Use the fitting building blocks:
- "alert": important warnings (variant "danger" for missables, "gold" for expert tips, "crystal" for info, "emerald" for achievements)
- "cards_grid": strategies, builds, tactics (columns 2 or 3; every card with bullets)
- "checklist_table": everything tickable (collectibles, quests, weapons, bosses ...), choose columns to fit the topic.
  Every row needs a unique, descriptive "itemId" in kebab-case (e.g. "weapon-mantis-blades"), "filterCategory"
  must appear in "categories"; "checkable" is true if the player can do/collect something.
- "puzzle": a riddle/sequence with a hidden solution – { "type": "puzzle", "title": "Name of the puzzle", "htmlContent": "The task in 1-2 sentences, no solution",
    "puzzle": { "itemId": "unique-id", "location": "Where exactly", "requirements": "What first / from when", "missable": false,
      "hints": ["vague hint", "more concrete hint"], "steps": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "source": "Name of the source" } }
- "boss": a boss card exactly where the fight happens (text only) – { "type": "boss", "bossId": "unique-kebab-id", "title": "Name of the boss",
    "location": "Where the fight takes place", "recommendedLevel": "e.g. level 25", "hp": [ { "value": "e.g. 12,000", "appliesTo": "difficulty/phase/version",
    "source": "Name of the source" } ], "weaknesses": ["..."], "resistances": ["..."], "immunities": ["..."],
    "attacks": [ { "name": "...", "note": "what it does, how to avoid it" } ], "phases": ["..."], "party": "recommended party/gear",
    "tactics": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "rewards": ["..."], "missable": false, "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "source": "Name of the source" }
- "sources": as the LAST section of the chapter the pages you really relied on – { "type": "sources", "sources": [ { "title": "Name of the page", "url": "https://..." } ] }.
  Only real, complete URLs you have actually seen; never invent a URL – without a URL leave the entry out.

Acquisition paths (mandatory rule): for everything that needs more than picking it up – weapons, legendary/ultimate gear, summons/espers,
special items, rewards of quest chains – describe the COMPLETE chain in the right order: the prerequisites and from when it is available,
what has to be collected, where to go (e.g. "the hall where you choose the weapon") and EVERY required fight – including a surprising, hard
fight at the end (warn about it BEFORE the fight and give it a "boss" card). No step may be missing, otherwise the player walks into a fight he
does not know about. One step per entry ("story" with "variant": "steps", or "puzzle.steps" for a riddle), the reward at the end.

Bosses (mandatory rule):
- Every boss fight and every especially hard mandatory fight gets its own "boss" card – in the walkthrough directly after the step that leads to the
  fight; in other chapters (bestiary, bosses, acquisition paths) where the fight belongs.
- Numbers only with evidence: every HP entry names the value, what it applies to ("appliesTo": difficulty, phase, version, NG+) and its source
  ("source"). Without a verified value write { "value": "Not documented" } – never invent numbers. Leave out every field you have nothing verified for.
- "tactics": the tactic as steps in whole sentences; "achievements": only exact Steam API names from the achievement list (if there is one), otherwise leave it out.
- "missable": true if the fight (or its reward) can be lost. Announce a surprise fight (no warning in the game) with an "alert" BEFORE it.

Puzzles and riddles (mandatory rule):
- EVERY puzzle, riddle, code, sequence, combination, hidden mechanism or task of the kind "do X in the right order" gets its own "puzzle"
  section – never just a vague sentence in a table cell, an alert or a card. Leave such a table cell short and name the puzzle there.
- WHERE exactly: "puzzle.location" (area, landmark, how to get there), "puzzle.requirements" (what has to be done or owned first, when it
  becomes available) and "puzzle.missable" (true if it can be lost). The visible task goes into "htmlContent" (one or two sentences, NO solution in it).
- The COMPLETE SOLUTION in "puzzle.steps": every step in the exact order, with the concrete answers, codes, symbols, positions, items and
  places – a player must be able to solve it with the list alone. Forbidden are sentences such as "solve the riddle" or "collect the 3
  fragments in the right order" without saying where each one is and which order is the right one.
- Optional "puzzle.hints": 1 to 3 hints from vague to concrete (no complete solution), so a player can ask for help bit by bit.
- "puzzle.source": where the solution comes from (site or guide name).
- If your research cannot verify the solution: leave "puzzle.steps" EMPTY (the app then shows "Solution not documented") and still give
  location and requirements as far as they are verified. NEVER invent codes, positions or orders.
- "puzzle.itemId" (kebab-case, unique) when the player can tick the puzzle off as done.

Platform (mandatory rule): the player plays the STEAM version on PC.
- Achievements are Steam achievements. Never write about PlayStation trophies, a platinum trophy, Xbox achievements or Gamerscore;
  "100%" means all Steam achievements (plus the in-game completion they need).
- Controls are keyboard and mouse (or a controller on PC). Never use console button names (Cross, Square, Triangle, R1, A/B/X/Y …) unless the
  PC version itself shows them.
- Leave out console-only content: console exclusives, console patch states, PSN/Xbox-only DLC, bonuses or events, cross-save, console
  performance modes. If the PC version differs (patch state, DLC, content), the PC version counts.

  identical on PC (story, route, missables, boss tactics, order) and are then not named as "PS4/PS5/PlayStation/Xbox guide" in the text – name the fact,

- Time estimates ("hours to 100%") are for all Steam achievements on PC – never "hours to the platinum trophy".

- Leave out console-only content: console exclusives, console patch states, PSN/Xbox-only DLC, bonuses or events, cross-save, console
  performance modes. If the PC version differs (patch state, DLC, content), the PC version counts.
- Console sites (PSNProfiles, PlayStationTrophies.org, PowerPyx trophy guides, TrueAchievements, Xbox guides …) may be used for everything that is
  identical on PC (story, route, missables, boss tactics, order) and are then not named as "PS4/PS5/PlayStation/Xbox guide" in the text – name the fact,
  not the console. The achievement list, the names and the conditions come ONLY from Steam: if a console trophy differs, Steam wins.
- Time estimates ("hours to 100%") are for all Steam achievements on PC – never "hours to the platinum trophy".

Language (mandatory rule): the whole guide is in English – every sentence, heading, table cell, step, objective and instruction.
- Write in your own words. Never copy sentences from sources in another language or from another language version of the game (also no
  objectives or quest texts) – rewrite them in English.
- Names of places, items, enemies, characters, quests and abilities: as they are called in the English version of the game, if the game has one
  (see "English version according to Steam" in the facts; without that line check it yourself). If there is no English version, use the in-game name
  and explain it briefly in English at its first mention.
- The original name in another language may be added in brackets at most ONCE per chapter, at its first mention – only for proper names, never for
  sentences, objectives or instructions, and never repeated. Chapter titles, headings and tab names get no bracketed addition.
- Steam API names, itemIds, URLs, codes and numbers stay exactly as they are.

Research rules:
- Step 1 – before all other sources: look for a wiki dedicated to THIS game, made by fans or official (for example the game's Fandom wiki, an official
  game wiki, or a fan wiki of the kind of ffresonance.wiki for Final Fantasy Resonance – many games have none). If it has an English version (often
  the default without a language path, otherwise a language switcher or a path like /en/), use that one. Name a wiki only when your search really
  found it; if there is none, skip this step without mentioning it. Never invent a wiki URL.
  Such wikis are often fan sites: cross-check numbers (HP, levels, drop rates, prices) with a second source where one exists and say so when they
  disagree; write in your own words and never copy their text; list every wiki page you used in the chapter's "sources" section (where the answer has one).
- Step 2 – research live on the internet. Good starting points (hints, keep searching on your own): Steam community guides (category Achievements),
  Game8 (walkthroughs), Fextralife (wikis), PowerPyx (roadmaps, collectibles), PSNProfiles and PlayStationTrophies.org (roadmaps, missables),
  official patch notes and Reddit.
- Invent NOTHING. If there is (as yet) no reliable source for a point – e.g. because the game has just been released –
  write explicitly "Not documented yet (as of <today's date>)" instead of guessing locations, values or names.
- Take the current patch state into account; mark content that has changed through updates.
- Keep it SHORT: only the most important and most common entries per topic (tables with at most about 15 of the most important rows), no complete lists – quality over quantity.
- For every entry state FROM WHEN it is available (chapter, area, level or story progress) and whether it is
  MISSABLE – the player wants to see: "I can already get this in this chapter, I have to watch out for this now."
- Write like an experienced player: concrete practical tips, time savers, typical mistakes, orders.
- Side quests: their complete solution (steps, fights, rewards) belongs ONLY in the side quest chapter. In the walkthrough a side quest is only
  named in a compact "alert" with "kind": "side_quests" at the spot where it becomes available – { "type": "alert", "kind": "side_quests", "title": "Side quests here",
  "quests": [ { "title": "Name", "availableFrom": "from when", "missable": "time window / until when (only if missable)", "reward": "short" } ] } – nothing more.
  In other chapters name the quest briefly and refer to the side quest chapter.
- Answer EXCLUSIVELY with a valid JSON object, without a Markdown code block and without text before or after it.

  "title": "Dorfausbau",
  "icon": "mdi:...",

<<<WISHES

- Collectibles, secrets, hidden places, easter eggs and secret achievements with exact location and condition.
- Missions, side tasks, activities, endings and decisions including rewards and consequences.
- Economy, resources, crafting and progression: the fastest ways to money, experience and important materials.

- The recommended path to 100 % or to all achievements: sensible order, number of playthroughs, time needed.

- Scope – compact: only the essentials per topic; short chapters, tables with the most important entries only (roughly 15 rows at most), no complete lists.
- Missables first: everything missable and every point of no return comes first (at the top of the chapter and first in every table), with the point in time and a save hint.

precedence, and the answer remains exclusively the requested JSON object.



Content: concise and concrete – real names, locations, conditions, numerical values, orders; only what matters most.
Structure (mandatory, in this order):
1. An "alert" (variant "gold") "Key points first": the 3–5 things you absolutely have to know about this topic.
2. An "alert" (variant "danger") with everything that is MISSABLE – or explicitly "Nothing missable".
3. "cards_grid" with strategies/recommendations from practice (e.g. early / middle / endgame, best build, best order).
4. One "checklist_table" with the most important entries; mandatory columns in addition to the topic-
   specific ones: "Available from" (chapter/area/level) and "Missable" (yes/no + until when).
Use the fitting building blocks:
- "alert": important warnings (variant "danger" for missables, "gold" for expert tips, "crystal" for info, "emerald" for achievements)
- "cards_grid": strategies, builds, tactics (columns 2 or 3; every card with bullets)
- "checklist_table": everything tickable (collectibles, quests, weapons, bosses ...), choose columns to fit the topic.
  Every row needs a unique, descriptive "itemId" in kebab-case (e.g. "weapon-mantis-blades"), "filterCategory"
  must appear in "categories"; "checkable" is true if the player can do/collect something.
- "puzzle": a riddle/sequence with a hidden solution – { "type": "puzzle", "title": "Name of the puzzle", "htmlContent": "The task in 1-2 sentences, no solution",
    "puzzle": { "itemId": "unique-id", "location": "Where exactly", "requirements": "What first / from when", "missable": false,
      "hints": ["vague hint", "more concrete hint"], "steps": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "source": "Name of the source" } }
- "boss": a boss card exactly where the fight happens (text only) – { "type": "boss", "bossId": "unique-kebab-id", "title": "Name of the boss",
    "location": "Where the fight takes place", "recommendedLevel": "e.g. level 25", "hp": [ { "value": "e.g. 12,000", "appliesTo": "difficulty/phase/version",
    "source": "Name of the source" } ], "weaknesses": ["..."], "resistances": ["..."], "immunities": ["..."],
    "attacks": [ { "name": "...", "note": "what it does, how to avoid it" } ], "phases": ["..."], "party": "recommended party/gear",
    "tactics": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "rewards": ["..."], "missable": false, "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "source": "Name of the source" }
- "sources": as the LAST section of the chapter the pages you really relied on – { "type": "sources", "sources": [ { "title": "Name of the page", "url": "https://..." } ] }.
  Only real, complete URLs you have actually seen; never invent a URL – without a URL leave the entry out.

Acquisition paths (mandatory rule): for everything that needs more than picking it up – weapons, legendary/ultimate gear, summons/espers,
special items, rewards of quest chains – describe the COMPLETE chain in the right order: the prerequisites and from when it is available,
what has to be collected, where to go (e.g. "the hall where you choose the weapon") and EVERY required fight – including a surprising, hard
fight at the end (warn about it BEFORE the fight and give it a "boss" card). No step may be missing, otherwise the player walks into a fight he
does not know about. One step per entry ("story" with "variant": "steps", or "puzzle.steps" for a riddle), the reward at the end.

Bosses (mandatory rule):
- Every boss fight and every especially hard mandatory fight gets its own "boss" card – in the walkthrough directly after the step that leads to the
  fight; in other chapters (bestiary, bosses, acquisition paths) where the fight belongs.
- Numbers only with evidence: every HP entry names the value, what it applies to ("appliesTo": difficulty, phase, version, NG+) and its source
  ("source"). Without a verified value write { "value": "Not documented" } – never invent numbers. Leave out every field you have nothing verified for.
- "tactics": the tactic as steps in whole sentences; "achievements": only exact Steam API names from the achievement list (if there is one), otherwise leave it out.
- "missable": true if the fight (or its reward) can be lost. Announce a surprise fight (no warning in the game) with an "alert" BEFORE it.

Puzzles and riddles (mandatory rule):
- EVERY puzzle, riddle, code, sequence, combination, hidden mechanism or task of the kind "do X in the right order" gets its own "puzzle"
  section – never just a vague sentence in a table cell, an alert or a card. Leave such a table cell short and name the puzzle there.

  becomes available) and "puzzle.missable" (true if it can be lost). The visible task goes into "htmlContent" (one or two sentences, NO solution in it).
- The COMPLETE SOLUTION in "puzzle.steps": every step in the exact order, with the concrete answers, codes, symbols, positions, items and
  places – a player must be able to solve it with the list alone. Forbidden are sentences such as "solve the riddle" or "collect the 3
  fragments in the right order" without saying where each one is and which order is the right one.
- Optional "puzzle.hints": 1 to 3 hints from vague to concrete (no complete solution), so a player can ask for help bit by bit.
- "puzzle.source": where the solution comes from (site or guide name).
- If your research cannot verify the solution: leave "puzzle.steps" EMPTY (the app then shows "Solution not documented") and still give
  location and requirements as far as they are verified. NEVER invent codes, positions or orders.
- "puzzle.itemId" (kebab-case, unique) when the player can tick the puzzle off as done.

First check whether there is a reliable, COMPLETE walkthrough source (large site, official guide, big wiki). If not: return "sections" with ONE

overview, areas with level 2, boss cards, typed hints exactly where they become relevant, the complete puzzle solutions). Keep it COMPACT enough
to fit into ONE answer (main route, the important hints only, no filler).
Structure of EVERY part (like an official printed strategy guide – only the structure, never its text):
1. "heading" with "level": 1 = the part (story chapter/mission); in "htmlContent" ONE short orientation sentence: where you are and what this part is about.
2. "overview" "At a glance": recommended level, how many collectibles/chests the part has ("counts"), the achievements of this part (API names),
   key items ("keyItems"), what is missable ("missables") and whether the part ends at a point of no return ("endsAtPointOfNoReturn").
3. Then one block per AREA/LOCATION in the order you pass through:
   a. "heading" with "level": 2 = the name of the area; in "htmlContent" one orientation sentence for this area.
   b. The route: "story" with "variant": "steps".
   c. What can be found in this area – only if it has such things: a "checklist_table" with the chests/collectibles/items of this area
      (tickable, unique "itemId", a column "Missable").
   d. Side quests that become available here: ONE compact "alert" with "kind": "side_quests" (see the side quest rule) – other optional content
      (optional boss, extra area): "alert" with "kind": "optional".
   e. A boss fight: a "boss" card directly after the step that leads to the fight.
   f. An achievement that unlocks here: "alert" with "kind": "achievement" and "apiName".
4. At the end of the part – before a point of no return – an "alert" with "kind": "point_of_no_return" and "items" (what you have to do before you continue).
Reading flow (mandatory rule):
- Every step is 1–2 complete sentences addressing the player as "you", e.g. "Go to the village of Kol and spend the night at the inn." – no telegram
  style, no "Objective:", no objective lists copied from the game or from sources.
- Merge trivial objectives (walking, a conversation, a cutscene) into one step. No numbering of your own.
- Typed hints (missable, point of no return, achievement, optional, important, tip) are their own "alert" exactly where they matter – never inside the step text.
Allowed building blocks (only these types):
- "heading": { "type": "heading", "level": 1, "title": "Part or area name", "htmlContent": "one orientation sentence" } ("level" 1 = part, 2 = area)
- "overview": "At a glance" at the start of a part – { "type": "overview", "title": "At a glance", "recommendedLevel": "e.g. 10–15",
    "counts": [ { "label": "Treasure chests", "count": 12 } ], "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "keyItems": ["..."], "missables": ["..."],
    "endsAtPointOfNoReturn": false } ("count" only with a verified number, otherwise leave it out)
- "story" with "variant": "steps": the route as steps – { "type": "story", "variant": "steps", "paragraphs": ["Go to ... and ...", "Then ..."] }
  (one step per entry, no numbering of your own).
- "checklist_table": the finds of an area (see "Structure" 3c), same shape as in the other chapters, with a unique "itemId" per row.
- "alert" with a "kind": a hint EXACTLY at the spot where it becomes relevant – { "type": "alert", "kind": "must_have | missable | optional | tip | achievement | point_of_no_return | side_quests",
  "title": "short heading", "htmlContent": "plain text" }:
  • "must_have" (Important – take it along): an item, equipment, ability or companion that matters for the run or for 100 % – with the exact location (where and how you get it) and why.
  • "missable" (Missable from here): from this point something is lost (time-limited content) – what exactly, until when; recommend saving beforehand.
  • "optional" (Optional content available here): optional boss, extra area, collectible that becomes available at this spot – and how to start it.
  • "side_quests" (Side quests here) with "quests": the side quests that become available here, compact (name, from when, missable window, short reward).
  • "tip" (Tip): practical advice for this spot (shortcut, save point, what to prepare).
  • "achievement" (Achievement here) with "apiName": the exact Steam API name – what you have to do here to unlock it.
  • "point_of_no_return" (Point of no return) with "items": ["..."] – everything you have to do before you continue.
- "boss": a boss card exactly where the fight happens (text only) – { "type": "boss", "bossId": "unique-kebab-id", "title": "Name of the boss",
    "location": "Where the fight takes place", "recommendedLevel": "e.g. level 25", "hp": [ { "value": "e.g. 12,000", "appliesTo": "difficulty/phase/version",
    "source": "Name of the source" } ], "weaknesses": ["..."], "resistances": ["..."], "immunities": ["..."],
    "attacks": [ { "name": "...", "note": "what it does, how to avoid it" } ], "phases": ["..."], "party": "recommended party/gear",
    "tactics": ["Step 1 ...", "Step 2 ..."], "rewards": ["..."], "missable": false, "achievements": ["API_NAME"], "source": "Name of the source" }

- Every boss fight and every especially hard mandatory fight gets its own "boss" card – in the walkthrough directly after the step that leads to the
  fight; in other chapters (bestiary, bosses, acquisition paths) where the fight belongs.

  ("source"). Without a verified value write { "value": "Not documented" } – never invent numbers. Leave out every field you have nothing verified for.

- "missable": true if the fight (or its reward) can be lost. Announce a surprise fight (no warning in the game) with an "alert" BEFORE it.

- EVERY puzzle, riddle, code, sequence, combination, hidden mechanism or task of the kind "do X in the right order" gets its own "puzzle"
  section – never just a vague sentence in a table cell, an alert or a card. Leave such a table cell short and name the puzzle there.

  write explicitly "Not documented yet (as of <today's date>)" instead of guessing locations, values or names.

  performance modes. If the PC version differs (patch state, DLC, content), the PC version counts.
- Console sites (PSNProfiles, PlayStationTrophies.org, PowerPyx trophy guides, TrueAchievements, Xbox guides …) may be used for everything that is
  identical on PC (story, route, missables, boss tactics, order) and are then not named as "PS4/PS5/PlayStation/Xbox guide" in the text – name the fact,
  not the console. The achievement list, the names and the conditions come ONLY from Steam: if a console trophy differs, Steam wins.
- Time estimates ("hours to 100%") are for all Steam achievements on PC – never "hours to the platinum trophy".

Language (mandatory rule): the whole guide is in English – every sentence, heading, table cell, step, objective and instruction.

  objectives or quest texts) – rewrite them in English.

  (see "English version according to Steam" in the facts; without that line check it yourself). If there is no English version, use the in-game name

  sentences, objectives or instructions, and never repeated. Chapter titles, headings and tab names get no bracketed addition.
- Steam API names, itemIds, URLs, codes and numbers stay exactly as they are.

Research rules:
- Step 1 – before all other sources: look for a wiki dedicated to THIS game, made by fans or official (for example the game's Fandom wiki, an official
  game wiki, or a fan wiki of the kind of ffresonance.wiki for Final Fantasy Resonance – many games have none). If it has an English version (often
  the default without a language path, otherwise a language switcher or a path like /en/), use that one. Name a wiki only when your search really
  found it; if there is none, skip this step without mentioning it. Never invent a wiki URL.

  disagree; write in your own words and never copy their text; list every wiki page you used in the chapter's "sources" section (where the answer has one).

  Game8 (walkthroughs), Fextralife (wikis), PowerPyx (roadmaps, collectibles), PSNProfiles and PlayStationTrophies.org (roadmaps, missables),
  official patch notes and Reddit.
- Invent NOTHING. If there is (as yet) no reliable source for a point – e.g. because the game has just been released –
  write explicitly "Not documented yet (as of <today's date>)" instead of guessing locations, values or names.
- Take the current patch state into account; mark content that has changed through updates.

    60 rows is better than one with 8 selected ones.

  MISSABLE – the player wants to see: "I can already get this in this chapter, I have to watch out for this now."
- Write like an experienced player: concrete practical tips, time savers, typical mistakes, orders.

  named in a compact "alert" with "kind": "side_quests" at the spot where it becomes available – { "type": "alert", "kind": "side_quests", "title": "Side quests here",

  In other chapters name the quest briefly and refer to the side quest chapter.

  PC version itself shows them.

  performance modes. If the PC version differs (patch state, DLC, content), the PC version counts.

  identical on PC (story, route, missables, boss tactics, order) and are then not named as "PS4/PS5/PlayStation/Xbox guide" in the text – name the fact,
  not the console. The achievement list, the names and the conditions come ONLY from Steam: if a console trophy differs, Steam wins.
- Time estimates ("hours to 100%") are for all Steam achievements on PC – never "hours to the platinum trophy".

Language (mandatory rule): the whole guide is in English – every sentence, heading, table cell, step, objective and instruction.
- Write in your own words. Never copy sentences from sources in another language or from another language version of the game (also no
  objectives or quest texts) – rewrite them in English.

  sentences, objectives or instructions, and never repeated. Chapter titles, headings and tab names get no bracketed addition.

  game wiki, or a fan wiki of the kind of ffresonance.wiki for Final Fantasy Resonance – many games have none). If it has an English version (often

  write explicitly "Not documented yet (as of <today's date>)" instead of guessing locations, values or names.
